    OPT   Est. Speedup: 44.08%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.5 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 44.1% of the total average of 28.4 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 43.69%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.3 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 43.7% of the total average of 28.2 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 37.92%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 9.1 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA     
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 37.9% of the total average of 23.9 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 37.9% of the total average of 23.9 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.5 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 44.2% of the total average of 28.3 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          instructions. When caused by shared memory accesses, trying to use fewer but wider loads can reduce pipeline  
          pressure. This stall type represents about 68.2% of the total average of 33.4 cycles between issuing two      
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 45.01%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.8 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 45.0% of the total average of 28.4 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 44.34%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.7 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 44.3% of the total average of 28.5 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 44.6% of the total average of 27.9 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          On average, each warp of this workload spends 9.0 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA     
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 37.5% of the total average of 24.0 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 44.8%                                                                                           
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.6 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 44.8% of the total average of 28.2 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 45.43%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.9 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 45.4% of the total average of 28.5 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 44.96%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.8 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 45.0% of the total average of 28.6 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 37.62%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 9.0 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA     
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 37.6% of the total average of 24.0 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          the MIO pipelines, which include special math instructions, dynamic branches, as well as shared memory

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 45.21%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.8 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 45.2% of the total average of 28.3 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          instructions. When caused by shared memory accesses, trying to use fewer but wider loads can reduce pipeline  
          pressure. This stall type represents about 66.5% of the total average of 33.8 cycles between issuing two      
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 44.65%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.6 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 44.7% of the total average of 28.3 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 45.36%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 13.0 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 45.4% of the total average of 28.6 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 37.7% of the total average of 24.0 cycles between issuing two

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 45.03%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 12.8 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 45.0% of the total average of 28.4 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 45.35%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 13.0 cycles being stalled waiting for sibling warps at a CTA    
          barrier. A high number of warps waiting at a barrier is commonly caused by diverging code paths before a      
          barrier. This causes some warps to wait a long time until other warps reach the synchronization point.        
          Whenever possible, try to divide up the work into blocks of uniform workloads. If the block size is 512       
          threads or greater, consider splitting it into smaller groups. This can increase eligible warps without       
          affecting occupancy, unless shared memory becomes a new occupancy limiter. Also, try to identify which        
          barrier instruction causes the most stalls, and optimize the code executed before that synchronization point  
          first. This stall type represents about 45.4% of the total average of 28.7 cycles between issuing two         
          instructions.
